# Sulfates

Units: I is in K; $Cp^{o}$ and $S^{o}$ are in cal/mol·K; $H^{o} - H_{298}^{o}$ , $\Delta Hf^{o}$ and $\Delta Gf^{o}$ are in kcal/mol.

All formation reactions for sulfates use $S_{2}(g)$ .

Sources of data: $Cs_{2}SO_{4}(c,1)$ and $Cs_{2}SO_{4}(g)$ (11), $K_{2}SO_{4}(c,1)$ , $K_{2}SO_{4}(g)$ , $Na_{2}SO_{4}(c,1)$ , and $Na_{2}SO_{4}(g)$ (3), $Li_{2}SO_{4}(c,1)$ and $Li_{2}SO_{4}(g)$ (9).

Data for all other sulfates were from

DeKock, C. W. Thermodynamic Properties of Selected Transition Metal Sulfates and Their Hydrates. BuMines IC 8910, 1982, 45 pp.

# Sulfides

Units: I is in K; $Cp^{o}$ and $S^{o}$ are in cal/mol·K; $H^{o} - H_{298}^{o}$ , $\Delta Hf^{o}$ and $\Delta Gf^{o}$ are in kcal/mol.

All formation reaction for sulfides use $S_{2}(g)$ . A formation table for $2S(c, l) = S_{2}(g)$ is included.

Sources of data from general references are listed below, with the reference(s) given immediately after each formula:

$Al_{2}S_{3}(c)$ (13), $AlS(g)$ (7), $AsS(g)$ (21), $As_{2}S_{2}(c,l)$ (23), $As_{2}S_{3}(orpiment, l)$ (21), $As_{2}S_{3}(gl,l)$ (21), $As_{4}S_{4}(realgar, l)$ (21), $As_{4}S_{4}(c,l)$ (21), $As_{4}S_{4}(g)$ (21), $BS(g)$ (4), $BaS(c)$ (3), $BaS(g)$ (3), $BeS(c)$ (3), $BeS(g)$ (3), $CaS(c)$ (4, 26), $CaS(g)$ (3), $CeS(c)$ (23), $Ce_{2}S_{3}(c)$ , (23), $CuFeS_{2}(c)$ (19), $Cu_{5}FeS_{4}(c)$ (19), $CuS(c)$ (19), $Cu_{2}S(c)$ (19), $Fe_{0.877}S(c)$ (3), $FeS(c,l)$ (17, 18), $FeS(g)$ (3), $FeS_{2}(pyrite)$ (3), $FeS_{2}(marcasite)$ (3), $GeS(c,l)$ (23), $GeS(g)$ (23), $GeS_{2}(c)$ (23), $HS(g)$ (23) $H_{2}S(g)$ (3, 30), $DS(g)$ (3), $D_{2}S(g)$ (3), $In_{2}S_{3}(c)$ (23), $K_{2}S(c,l)$ (3), $La_{2}S_{3}(c)$ (23), $MgS(c)$ (3), $MgS(g)$ (3), $MnS(c,l)$ (23), $MoS_{2}(c)$ (3, 8), $Mo_{2}S_{3}(c,l)$ (3, 8), $Na_{2}S(c,l)$ (3), $NdS(c)$ (23), $NiS(c,l)$ (22), $NiS_{2}(c)$ (3), $Ni_{3}S_{4}(c)$ (3), $Ni_{2}S_{3}(c,l)$ (22), $PS(g)$ (7), $P_{4}S_{3}(c,l)$ (23), $P_{4}S_{3}(g)$ (7), $PbS(g)$ (6), $Pd_{4}S(c)$ (23), $PtS(c)$ (23), $PtS_{2}(c)$ (23), $SiS(g)$ (4), $SiS_{2}(c,l)$ (23), $SrS(c)$ (3), $Sr(S(c)$ (3), $TiS(g)$ (23), $US(c)$ (23), $ZnS(\beta)$ (23), $ZnSO_{4}(c)$ (10).

# Tellurides

Units: I is in K; $Cp^{\circ}$ and $S^{\circ}$ are in cal/mol·K; $H^{\circ} - H_{298}^{\circ}$ , $\Delta Hf^{\circ}$ and $\Delta Gf^{\circ}$ are in kcal/mol.

All formation reactions for tellurides use $\mathrm{Te}_{2}(g)$ . A formation table for $2\mathrm{Te}(c,l)=\mathrm{Te}_{2}(g)$ is included.

Most data were from reference 23.

Additional sources:

$As_{2}Te_{3}(c)$ :

Medzhidov, R. A., and S. M. Rasulov. Bysokotemperaturnye Entalpiya, Teploemkost, Entropiya i Izobarno - Izotermicheskii Potentsial As $_{2}$ Te $_{3}$ (High-Temperature Enthalpy, Heat Capacity, Entropy, and Gibbs Free Energy of As $_{2}$ Te $_{3}$ ). Vses. Inst. Nauchnoi i Tekh. Inf., Moscow, Doc. 3343-74, 1974, 7 pp.

Zhdanov, V. M., and A. K. Mal'tsev. Low Temperature Heat Capacity, Enthalpy, and Entropy of Crystalline and Vitreous Arsenic Sesquiselenide and of Crystalline Arsenic Sesquitelluride. Russ. J. Phys. Chem. (Engl. Transl.), v. 42, 1968, pp. 1078-1080.

CuTe(c) and $\mathbf{Cu}_2\mathbf{Te}(\mathbf{c})$

Abbasov, A. S., I. Kh. Azizov, N. A. Alieva, I. Ya. Aliev, F. M. Mustafaev, and A. N. Mamedov. Thermodynamic Properties of Copper Tellurides. Russ. J. Phys. Chem. (Engl. Transl.), v. 50, 1976, p. 1309.

Mills, K. C., and M. J. Richardson. Heat Capacity of Copper(I) Telluride(c), Copper(II) Telluride, and Silver Telluride(c) (Ag $_{1.64}$ Te). Thermochim. Acta, v. 6, 1973, pp. 427-438.

EuTe(c):

McMasters, O. D., K. A. Gschneidner, Jr., E. Kaldis, and G. Sampietro. High-Temperature Enthalpies and Standard Gibbs Free Energies of Formation of the Europium Chalcogenides: EuO, EuS, EuSe, and EuTe. J. Chem. Thermodyn., v. 6, 1974, pp. 845-857.

GaTe(c,1):

Kerimov, I. G., K. K. Mamedov, M. I. Mekhtiev, and V. N. Kostryukov. Low-Temperature Specific Heats of Gallium and Indium Tellurides. Russ. J. Phys. Chem. (Engl. Transl.), v. 45, 1971, pp. 1118-1119.

Said, H., and R. Castanet. Thermodynamic Investigations of Liquid and Solid Ga-Te Alloys. J. Less-Common Met., v. 68, 1979, pp. 213-221.

GeTe(c,1):

Castanet, R., and C. Bergman. Capacite Calorifique Du Compose $Ge_{0.5}Te_{0.5}$ A L'Etat Liquide et Solide (Heat Capacity of $Ge_{0.5}Te_{0.5}$ in the Solid and Liquid State). J. Less-Common Met., v. 68, 1979, pp. 119-124.

Zhdanov, V. M. Low-Temperature Heat Capacity, Enthalpy, and Entropy of Germanium Telluride. Russ. J. Phys. Chem. (Engl. Transl.), v. 45, 1971, p. 1357.

MnIe(c):

Gronvold, F., N. J. Kveseth, F. D. S. Marques, and J. Tichy. Thermophysical Properties of Manganese Monotelluride From 298 to 700 K. Lattice Constants, Magnetic Susceptibility, and Antiferromagnetic Transition. J. Chem. Thermodyn., v. 4, 1972, pp. 795-806.

Kelley, K. K. Specific Heats at Low Temperatures of Manganese, Manganous Selenide, and Manganous Telluride. J. Am. Chem. Soc., v. 61, 1939, p. 203.

RuTe $_{2}$ (c):

Svendsen, S. R. Decomposition Pressures and Thermodynamic Properties of RuTe₂. J. Chem. Thermodyn., v. 9, 1977, pp. 789-800.

SnTe(c,1):

Medzhidov, R. A., and S. M. Rasulov. Entalpiya i Teploemkost SnTe pri Vysokikh Temperatura v Iverdom i Shidkom Sostoyaniyakh (Enthalpy and Heat Capacity of SnTe at High Temperatures in the Solid and Liquid State). Prikl. Fiz. Iverd. Tela, 1973, pp. 127-133.
